乌干达西部布戈耶县 患病率 和与乙型肝炎表面抗原 (HBsAg) 阳性相关的因素

Prevalence and factors associated with hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) positivity in Bugoye Sub-County, Western Uganda
作者： Brian Turigye, Edgar M. Mulogo, Peyton Thompson, Morgan Camille, Varun Goel, Emmanuel Baguma, Khadijah Bhatti, Ross M. Boyce
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染是全球发病率和 死亡率 的主要原因。全国血清调查表明,乌干达慢性 HBV 感染的 患病率 为 4.3%。然而,关于感染的潜在决定因素的信息有限。方法 使用来自卡塞塞地区人口家庭调查的二手数据来估计 HBsAg 阳性率。根据这些结果,有目的地选择了两个村庄(即低 患病率 和高 患病率)进行人口代表性住户调查和描述性横断面研究。根据人口、社会行为、临床和卫生系统因素进行描述性分析来表征 HBsAg 阳性,并使用空间核密度分析和扫描统计来检测高风险区域。结果 共有 2,067 名成年人接受了 HBV 检测,其中 93 名 HBsAg 阳性,总体 血清流行率 为 4.5%(95% CI:3.6-5.5)。在两个选定的村庄中,共招募了 286 名参与者,其中 125 名年龄 ≥ 15 岁的个体和 161 名年龄 < 15 岁的儿童。125 名成人中有 6 名 (4.8%) 检测出 HBsAg 阳性,没有儿童检测到。描述性比较表明,HBsAg 阳性参与者更频繁地报告 HBV 检测、输血和接受肠外药物的病史。在研究区域的中心区域发现了一个显着的 HBV 风险升高的空间集群(RR = 5.22,p = 0.0266)。结论 乌干达西部农村各村庄之间的 HBsAg 患病率 差异很大。这表明,感染仅发生在成年人中。这表明 传播 肝炎对儿童有好处,并且可能表明垂直和早年 传播 的危险因素可能较小。在患有 HBV 的成人中,我们观察到先前与医疗保健相关的暴露频率较高,这值得进一步研究旨在预防血源性病原体 传播 的做法。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述调查了乌干达西部布戈耶县的人类 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染情况,重点关注成人和儿童中 HBsAg 阳性的 患病率 以及相关的人口统计、社会行为、临床和卫生系统因素。
证据基础与方法
该评价使用乌干达卡塞塞地区基于人口的家庭调查的二手数据,采用描述性横断面设计和有目的的村庄选择(低和高 患病率)和空间分析来表征 HBsAg 阳性率。
共识与结论
成人 HBsAg 血清流行率 总体为 4.5%(95% CI:3.6-5.5),未发现儿童病例。HBsAg 阳性参与者更频繁地报告先前的 HBV 检测、输血和接受肠外药物。在研究区域的中心区域发现了一个显着的 HBV 风险升高的空间集群(RR = 5.22,p = 0.0266)。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了乌干达乙型肝炎病毒感染决定因素的信息有限,特别是缺乏垂直和早期 传播 动态数据。成人 HBsAg 阳性的独家检测表明儿童可能存在漏报或诊断不足的情况,需要进一步调查。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,应优先考虑儿童中的乙型肝炎 传播,并加强感染控制实践,以预防与医疗保健相关的 HBV 传播,特别是在高风险空间集群中。

Prevalence and factors associated with hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) positivity in Bugoye Sub-County, Western Uganda

Authors: Brian Turigye, Edgar M. Mulogo, Peyton Thompson, Morgan Camille, Varun Goel, Emmanuel Baguma, Khadijah Bhatti, Ross M. Boyce
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection is a major cause of global morbidity and mortality. National serosurveys suggest that Uganda has a prevalence of 4.3%of chronic HBV infection. However, there is limited information regarding the potential determinants for infection. Methods Secondary data from a population-based household survey in the Kasese District were used to estimate HBsAg positivity. From these results, two villages were purposefully selected (i.e., low and high prevalence) for a population-representative household survey and a descriptive cross-sectional study. Descriptive analyses were conducted to characterise HBsAg positivity according to demographic, social-behavioural, clinical, and health-system factors, and spatial kernel density analysis and scan statistics were used to detect high-risk areas. Results A total of 2,067 adults were tested for HBV, of whom 93 were HBsAg-positive, giving an overall seroprevalence of 4.5% (95% CI: 3.6–5.5). In the two selected villages, 286 participants were enrolled, including 125 individuals aged ≥ 15 years and 161 children aged < 15 years. HBsAg positivity was detected in 6 of 125 adults (4.8%) and in no children. Descriptive comparisons suggested that HBsAg-positive participants more frequently reported a history of HBV testing, blood transfusion, and receipt of parenteral medications. A significant spatial cluster of elevated HBV risk was identified in the central region of the study area (RR = 5.22, p = 0.0266). Conclusions The prevalence of HBsAg is highly variable between villages in rural western Uganda. Infection was exclusively found in adults, which suggests. This suggests the benefit of hepatitis vaccination among children and could suggest that vertical and early-life transmission may be less of a risk factor. In adults with HBV, we observed a higher frequency of prior healthcare-associated exposures, which merits further investigation into practices intended to prevent transmission of blood-borne pathogens.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection in Bugoye Sub-County, Western Uganda, focusing on the prevalence of HBsAg positivity and associated demographic, social-behavioural, clinical, and health-system factors among adults and children.
Evidence base and method
The review uses secondary data from a population-based household survey in Kasese District, Uganda, employing a descriptive cross-sectional design with purposeful village selection (low and high prevalence) and spatial analysis to characterize HBsAg positivity.
Consensus and conclusions
The overall HBsAg seroprevalence in adults was 4.5% (95% CI: 3.6–5.5), with no detected cases in children. HBsAg-positive participants more frequently reported prior HBV testing, blood transfusion, and receipt of parenteral medications. A significant spatial cluster of elevated HBV risk was identified in the central region of the study area (RR = 5.22, p = 0.0266).
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights limited information on determinants of HBV infection in Uganda, particularly the lack of data on vertical and early-life transmission dynamics. The exclusive detection of HBsAg positivity in adults suggests potential underreporting or underdiagnosis in children, warranting further investigation.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest prioritizing hepatitis B vaccination among children and strengthening infection control practices to prevent healthcare-associated HBV transmission, particularly in high-risk spatial clusters.

What is the impact of metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) on outcomes in people living with chronic hepatitis B (CHB)? An umbrella review protocol
作者： Emily Martyn, Jessica Carter, Maeve Barlow, Flora Olcott, Indrajit Ghosh, Elizabeth Waddilove, Alejandro Arenas-Pinto, Richard Gilson, Stuart Flanagan, WD Francois Venter
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的目标是代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪性肝病 (MASLD) 对 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 患者肝脏和病毒结果的影响。

What is the impact of metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) on outcomes in people living with chronic hepatitis B (CHB)? An umbrella review protocol

Authors: Emily Martyn, Jessica Carter, Maeve Barlow, Flora Olcott, Indrajit Ghosh, Elizabeth Waddilove, Alejandro Arenas-Pinto, Richard Gilson, Stuart Flanagan, WD Francois Venter
Original abstract (English)
Introduction An estimated 254 million people are living with chronic hepatitis B virus (CHB) and 1.6 billion with metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) worldwide. CHB remains a major global health challenge, despite a robust vaccine and suppressive antiviral treatment, and MASLD prevalence is increasing in line with a rise in cardiometabolic risk factors, such as obesity and type 2 diabetes. Thus, understanding the impact of concurrent CHB and MASLD on liver health is important to inform clinical practice and population surveillance. Current literature, including over ten systematic reviews and meta-analyses, yields conflicting evidence about the interplay between CHB and MASLD. This protocol describes a study aiming to critically appraise published systematic reviews investigating the effect of MASLD on liver outcomes in people living with CHB, identify gaps in the available data and assist scientists and clinicians in informing future research design. Methods and analysis We will use umbrella review methodology from the Joanna Briggs Institute (JBI) to evaluate and synthesise evidence from existing systematic reviews and meta-analysis investigating liver and viral outcomes (fibrosis, cirrhosis, hepatocellular carcinoma, response to antiviral treatment, and impact on CHB biomarkers e.g. hepatitis B surface antigen, hepatitis B e-antigen and viral load) in CHB and MASLD, compared to CHB alone. We will search Ovid Medline, Ovid Embase, PubMed and Web of Science. We will assess methodological quality using the Assessing the Quality of Systematic Reviews-2 (AMSTAR-2) tool, compare study characteristics and present important results. Ethics and dissemination No ethics approval is required as we will use only data from systematic reviews that are already published. Results will be included in a PhD thesis, submitted for consideration for presentation at international conferences and for peer review for publication in a scientific journal. We will endeavour to share results with relevant public and patient organisations. Registration details This umbrella review has been published on PROSPERO (ID CRD420261308282).
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review targets the effect of metabolic dysfunction‐associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) on liver and viral outcomes in people with chronic hepatitis B (CHB).
Evidence base and method
An umbrella review will be conducted following JBI methodology, searching Ovid Medline, Embase, PubMed and Web of Science, assessing systematic reviews with AMSTAR‐2, and registered on PROSPERO (CRD420261308282).
Consensus and conclusions
The abstract notes that existing systematic reviews provide conflicting evidence on the CHB‐MASLD interplay, indicating no clear consensus yet.
Controversies and gaps
Current literature shows heterogeneity and contradictory findings, and the protocol seeks to identify data gaps regarding MASLD’s impact on CHB outcomes.
Research and practice implications
Understanding MASLD’s influence on CHB is deemed important for clinical practice and population surveillance, and the review aims to guide future research design and inform clinicians.

脂质代谢重塑肝细胞癌的免疫景观:从机制到治疗。

Lipid metabolism remodeling the immunological landscape of hepatocellular carcinoma: From mechanisms to therapeutics.
作者： Kaitong Mai, Junqing Jiang, Xianglong Ma, Wenjie Huang, Limin Xia
摘要中文翻译肝细胞癌(HCC)仍然是全球主要的恶性肿瘤,具有高 发病率 和低生存率。病因驱动的代谢-免疫相互作用已成为破译肝癌发生的统一框架。乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪肝病 (MASLD) 是 HCC 的两个主要病因驱动因素。尽管免疫疗法是 HCC 治疗 的基石,但不同病因的治疗反应差异很大。越来越多的证据表明,脂质代谢的病因特异性失调会导致肿瘤微环境中的免疫失衡,从而解释对免疫治疗的异质反应。因此,破译病因驱动的代谢免疫串扰可能为改善治疗反应提供新的机会。在这篇综述中,我们通过比较两种主要病因(HBV 和 MASLD),提出了病因驱动的代谢-免疫相互作用的框架。此外,考虑到脂质代谢在肝癌发生中的关键作用,饮食干预和脂质代谢靶向治疗也被纳入其中,以提供潜在的治疗策略。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了肝细胞癌 (HCC) 背景下的人类 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染,重点关注 HBV 作为主要病因驱动因素及其在疾病 发病机制 和治疗反应变异中的作用。
证据基础与方法
该综述采用比较 HBV 与代谢功能障碍相关脂肪性肝病 (MASLD) 的叙述框架,探索病因驱动的代谢-免疫相互作用,将饮食干预和脂质代谢靶向治疗结合起来作为潜在的治疗策略。
共识与结论
HBV 被认为是 HCC 的两个主要病因驱动因素之一,越来越多的证据表明,脂质代谢的病因特异性失调会导致肿瘤微环境中的免疫失衡,从而导致异质性免疫治疗反应。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了不同病因(包括 HBV 相关 HCC)免疫治疗反应中尚未解决的异质性,但没有对这些变化提供汇总估计或明确的机制解释。
科研与实践启示
破译病因驱动的代谢免疫串扰,特别是在 HBV 相关的 HCC 中,被建议作为改善治疗反应的优先事项,并建议将饮食干预和脂质代谢靶向治疗作为潜在策略。

Lipid metabolism remodeling the immunological landscape of hepatocellular carcinoma: From mechanisms to therapeutics.

Authors: Kaitong Mai, Junqing Jiang, Xianglong Ma, Wenjie Huang, Limin Xia
Original abstract (English)
Hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) remains a major global malignancy with high incidence and poor survival rates. Etiology-driven metabolic-immune interactions have emerged as a unifying framework for deciphering hepatocarcinogenesis. Hepatitis B virus (HBV) and metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) are the two predominant etiological drivers for HCC. Although immunotherapy serves as a cornerstone of HCC treatment, therapeutic response varies considerably across distinct etiologies. Accumulating evidence suggests that etiology-specific dysregulation of lipid metabolism shapes immune imbalance in the tumor microenvironment, thereby accounting for the heterogeneous responses to immunotherapy. Therefore, deciphering etiology-driven metabolic-immune crosstalk may provide new opportunities to improve therapeutic responses. In this review, we propose the framework of etiology-driven metabolic-immune interactions by comparing two major etiologies, HBV and MASLD. Moreover, taking into account the crucial role of lipid metabolism in hepatocarcinogenesis, dietary interventions and lipid metabolism-targeted therapies have also been included so as to offer potential therapeutic strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines human hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection within the context of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC), focusing on HBV as a predominant etiological driver and its role in disease pathogenesis and therapeutic response variability.
Evidence base and method
The review adopts a narrative framework comparing HBV with metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) to explore etiology-driven metabolic-immune interactions, integrating dietary interventions and lipid metabolism-targeted therapies as potential therapeutic strategies.
Consensus and conclusions
HBV is identified as one of the two predominant etiological drivers of HCC, with accumulating evidence indicating that etiology-specific dysregulation of lipid metabolism shapes immune imbalance in the tumor microenvironment, contributing to heterogeneous immunotherapy responses.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights unresolved heterogeneity in immunotherapy responses across distinct etiologies, including HBV-associated HCC, without providing pooled estimates or definitive mechanistic explanations for these variations.
Research and practice implications
Deciphering etiology-driven metabolic-immune crosstalk, particularly in HBV-associated HCC, is proposed as a priority to improve therapeutic responses, with dietary interventions and lipid metabolism-targeted therapies suggested as potential strategies.

处于衰老和癌症十字路口的肿瘤病毒:分子机制和关键信号通路。

Oncoviruses at the Crossroads of Ageing and Cancer: Molecular Mechanisms and Key Signalling Pathways.
作者： Noor Allehyani, Mohammed Alissa, Abdullah Alghamdi, Mohammed A. Alshehri, Ghadah S. Abusalim, Tawfiq N. Juraybi, Meshari A. Alsuwat, Awaji Y. Safhi
摘要中文翻译肿瘤病毒是导致大部分人类癌症的原因,并且已成为 宿主 细胞途径的关键调节剂,该途径也控制着衰老。病毒肿瘤发生、衰老和癌症的融合反映了共同的分子机制,包括基因组不稳定性、表观遗传改变、端粒磨损和慢性炎症。越来越多的证据表明,人乳头瘤病毒、EB 病毒、乙型和丙型肝炎病毒以及卡波西肉瘤相关疱疹病毒等肿瘤病毒会主动重编程 宿主 信号网络,以促进持续感染、免疫逃避和恶性转化。这篇综述综合了目前关于肿瘤病毒如何与衰老和癌症的关键特征相交叉的知识,特别强调了桥接这些过程的信号通路。包括 p53、mTOR、NF-κB、PI3K/AKT 和 Wnt/β-catenin 在内的中央通路经常成为病毒蛋白的目标,导致细胞存活、增殖和衰老失调。这些相互作用不仅促进肿瘤发生,而且加速与衰老相关的细胞功能障碍,例如免疫衰老和炎症。此外,我们讨论了病毒诱导的肿瘤微环境调节和 宿主 免疫应答 如何促进衰老与癌症之间的动态相互作用。了解这些相互关联的途径可以为了解肿瘤病毒作为与年龄相关的衰退和癌症发展的驱动因素的双重作用提供有价值的见解。阐明这些机制可能会揭示预防和治疗老龄化人群中与病毒相关的恶性肿瘤的新治疗靶点和策略。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该范围检查人类 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染及其疾病表现,包括 慢性乙型肝炎、急性乙型肝炎、HBV 相关肝细胞癌和 HBV 相关肝硬化。核心问题涉及 HBV 如何与与衰老和癌症相关的 宿主 细胞通路交叉,重点关注共享的分子机制和信号网络。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一种叙述性综合,整合了有关肿瘤病毒、衰老和癌症的当前知识,没有指定数据库、搜索日期、资格标准或选择方法。它不报告纳入研究计数、荟萃分析或共识过程。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,HBV 与其他肿瘤病毒一样,主动重新编程 宿主 信号通路,例如 p53、mTOR、NF-κB、PI3K/AKT 和 Wnt/β-连环蛋白,导致细胞存活、增殖和衰老失调。这些相互作用会导致肿瘤发生和衰老相关的细胞功能障碍,包括免疫衰老和炎症。
争议与缺口
该综述没有提供有关 HBV 基因型的特定目标证据,也没有提供将 HBV 与衰老途径联系起来的详细机制研究。它缺乏对 HBV 基因型、人群或地区异质性的讨论,也没有解决 HBV 特有的未解决的机制。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要阐明连接衰老和癌症的 HBV 特异性机制,以确定预防和治疗老龄化人群中 HBV 相关恶性肿瘤的新治疗靶点和策略。

Oncoviruses at the Crossroads of Ageing and Cancer: Molecular Mechanisms and Key Signalling Pathways.

Authors: Noor Allehyani, Mohammed Alissa, Abdullah Alghamdi, Mohammed A. Alshehri, Ghadah S. Abusalim, Tawfiq N. Juraybi, Meshari A. Alsuwat, Awaji Y. Safhi
Original abstract (English)
Oncoviruses are responsible for a substantial fraction of human cancers and have emerged as critical modulators of host cellular pathways that also govern ageing. The convergence of viral oncogenesis, ageing, and cancer reflects shared molecular mechanisms, including genomic instability, epigenetic alterations, telomere attrition, and chronic inflammation. Increasing evidence indicates that oncoviruses such as human papillomavirus, Epstein-Barr virus, hepatitis B and C viruses, and Kaposi's sarcoma-associated herpesvirus actively reprogramme host signalling networks to promote persistent infection, immune evasion, and malignant transformation. This review synthesises current knowledge on how oncoviruses intersect with key hallmarks of ageing and cancer, with particular emphasis on the signalling pathways that bridge these processes. Central pathways, including p53, mTOR, NF-κB, PI3K/AKT, and Wnt/β-catenin, are frequently targeted by viral proteins, leading to dysregulated cell survival, proliferation, and senescence. These interactions not only facilitate tumourigenesis but also accelerate ageing-associated cellular dysfunctions, such as immunosenescence and inflammaging. Furthermore, we discuss how virus-induced modulation of the tumour microenvironment and host immune responses contributes to the dynamic interplay between ageing and cancer. Understanding these interconnected pathways provides valuable insights into the dual role of oncoviruses as drivers of both age-related decline and cancer development. Elucidating these mechanisms may reveal novel therapeutic targets and strategies for preventing and treating virus-associated malignancies in ageing populations.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The scope examines human hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection and its disease manifestations, including chronic hepatitis B, acute hepatitis B, HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma, and HBV-related cirrhosis. The central question addresses how HBV intersects with host cellular pathways linked to ageing and cancer, focusing on shared molecular mechanisms and signalling networks.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis that integrates current knowledge on oncoviruses, ageing, and cancer without specifying databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or selection methods. It does not report an included-study count, meta-analysis, or consensus process.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that HBV, alongside other oncoviruses, actively reprograms host signalling pathways such as p53, mTOR, NF-κB, PI3K/AKT, and Wnt/β-catenin, leading to dysregulated cell survival, proliferation, and senescence. These interactions contribute to both tumourigenesis and ageing-associated cellular dysfunctions, including immunosenescence and inflammaging.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not provide target-specific evidence on HBV genotypes or detailed mechanistic studies linking HBV to ageing pathways. It lacks discussion of heterogeneity across HBV genotypes, populations, or regions, and does not address unresolved mechanisms specific to HBV.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need to elucidate HBV-specific mechanisms that bridge ageing and cancer to identify novel therapeutic targets and strategies for preventing and treating HBV-associated malignancies in ageing populations.

抗病毒药物 治疗和诊断中 CRISPR/Cas 系统的新兴技术:应用、局限性和转化视角。

Emerging Techniques of CRISPR/Cas System in Antiviral Therapy and Diagnostics: Applications, Limitations, and Translational Perspectives.
作者： Mahmood Ul Hasan, Muhammad Waleed Azhar, Noor-Ul-Saba, Marriam Khurshid, Rabia Riaz
摘要中文翻译CRISPR/Cas(成簇规则间隔短回文重复序列)系统是一种多功能技术,用于开发 抗病毒药物 药物以及在诊断和 疫苗 合成中编辑病毒 基因组。对针对病毒 DNA 和 RNA 的 Cas9、Cas12 和 Cas13 等 2 类效应器的新见解已经彻底改变了 疫苗 对抗 HIV、HPV、HBV 和 EBV 等病毒的能力。SHERLOCK、DETECTR、FELUDA等创新诊断技术展示了系统在检测病毒标志物、支持临床决策方面的多样性和准确性,表明了CRISPR的适应性和精确性。这篇综述批判性地评估了 CRISPR 在 RNA 编辑中的作用,强调了它对功能基因组学和重组 疫苗 开发的重要性。批判性地讨论了转化挑战,包括脱靶效应、递送限制和伦理问题,针对这些挑战,评估了高保真 Cas 变体、非病毒递送系统和生物伦理框架等独特方法来解决这些限制。本综述还涵盖了与 CRISPR 技术相关的其他社会影响,例如可及性和生物安全风险。总的来说,这些进步强调了 CRISPR 技术在塑造下一代 抗病毒药物 诊断和治疗方面的变革潜力。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述研究了 CRISPR/Cas 系统作为 抗病毒药物 治疗和诊断的工具,重点关注其对抗人类 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 感染的潜在应用,包括其基因型和相关疾病,如 慢性乙型肝炎、急性乙型肝炎、HBV 相关肝细胞癌和 HBV 相关肝硬化。
证据基础与方法
该综述是一篇叙述性综述,评估了 2 类 CRISPR/Cas 效应器(Cas9、Cas12、Cas13)及其在靶向病毒 DNA 和 RNA 中的作用,重点是 HBV 和其他病毒,如 HIV、HPV、HBV 和 EBV。它讨论了转化挑战,包括脱靶效应、交付限制和伦理问题,但没有报告系统搜索方法、数据库、资格标准或研究计数。
共识与结论
CRISPR/Cas 系统,特别是 Cas9、Cas12 和 Cas13,在 抗病毒药物 应用中展示了多功能性,包括 基因组 编辑和诊断。它强调了基于 CRISPR 的诊断技术(例如 SHERLOCK、DETECTR 和 FELUDA)用于检测病毒标记物的适应性和精确性,从而支持临床决策。
争议与缺口
该审查确定了尚未解决的重大转化挑战,包括脱靶效应、交付限制和道德问题。它还指出,虽然 CRISPR 系统对 HBV 和其他病毒靶点显示出希望,但 HBV 特异性应用的证据基础尚未系统合成,并且临床环境中的长期安全性和有效性尚未确定。
科研与实践启示
该审查优先考虑开发高保真 Cas 变体和非病毒递送系统,以减轻脱靶效应并提高递送效率。它还强调需要建立生物伦理框架来解决伦理问题,并呼吁进一步研究以在临床实践中验证基于 CRISPR 的 HBV 诊断和治疗方法。

Emerging Techniques of CRISPR/Cas System in Antiviral Therapy and Diagnostics: Applications, Limitations, and Translational Perspectives.

Authors: Mahmood Ul Hasan, Muhammad Waleed Azhar, Noor-Ul-Saba, Marriam Khurshid, Rabia Riaz
Original abstract (English)
The CRISPR/Cas (clustered regularly interspaced short palindromic repeats) system is a versatile technology for developing antiviral medicines and editing viral genomes in both diagnostics and vaccine synthesis. Emerging insights into class 2 effectors, such as Cas9, Cas12, and Cas13, which target viral DNA and RNA, have revolutionized vaccines against viruses such as HIV, HPV, HBV, and EBV. Innovative diagnostic techniques such as SHERLOCK, DETECTR, and FELUDA have demonstrated system's diversity and accuracy in detecting the virus markers, supporting clinical decision-making, indicating adaptability and precision of CRISPR. This review critically evaluates CRISPR's role in RNA editing, emphasizing its importance for functional genomics and development of recombinant vaccines. Translational challenges are critically discussed, including off-target effects, delivery limitations, and ethical issues, for which unique approaches such as high-fidelity Cas variants, non-viral delivery systems, and bioethical frameworks are evaluated to address these limitations. This review also covers other social implications, such as accessibility and biosecurity risks, associated with CRISPR technologies Collectively, these advances underscore the transformative potential of CRISPR technologies in shaping next-generation antiviral diagnostics and therapeutics.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the CRISPR/Cas system as a tool for antiviral therapy and diagnostics, with a focus on its potential applications against human hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, including its genotypes and associated diseases such as chronic hepatitis B, acute hepatitis B, HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma, and HBV-related cirrhosis.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis that evaluates class 2 CRISPR/Cas effectors (Cas9, Cas12, Cas13) and their roles in targeting viral DNA and RNA, with emphasis on HBV and other viruses such as HIV, HPV, HBV, and EBV. It discusses translational challenges including off-target effects, delivery limitations, and ethical issues, but does not report systematic search methods, databases, eligibility criteria, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that CRISPR/Cas systems, particularly Cas9, Cas12, and Cas13, demonstrate versatility in antiviral applications, including genome editing and diagnostics. It highlights the adaptability and precision of CRISPR-based diagnostic techniques such as SHERLOCK, DETECTR, and FELUDA for detecting viral markers, which support clinical decision-making.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies significant translational challenges, including off-target effects, delivery limitations, and ethical concerns, which remain unresolved. It also notes that while CRISPR systems show promise for HBV and other viral targets, the evidence base for HBV-specific applications is not systematically synthesized, and the long-term safety and efficacy in clinical settings are not established.
Research and practice implications
The review prioritizes the development of high-fidelity Cas variants and non-viral delivery systems to mitigate off-target effects and improve delivery efficiency. It also emphasizes the need for bioethical frameworks to address ethical concerns and calls for further research to validate CRISPR-based diagnostics and therapeutics specifically for HBV in clinical practice.

预防乙型肝炎垂直 传播 - Contagion Live

Preventing Hepatitis B Vertical Transmission - Contagion Live
新闻简报2025 年《柳叶刀全球健康》荟萃分析估计,2022 年,世卫组织非洲地区有 172,000 名婴儿通过母婴 传播 感染乙型肝炎,其中 6.2% 的孕妇 HBsAg 呈阳性。四分之一的慢性感染婴儿面临因肝癌或并发症而过早死亡的情况。目前出生剂量 传播 覆盖率仅为 17%,远低于将 传播 削减 44% 所需的 90% 目标。将 疫苗接种 与孕产妇 抗病毒药物 预防相结合,可以使 传播 减少 86% 以上,但获取仍然受到成本(在刚果民主共和国,替诺福韦的费用为每月 25-30 美元)和后勤障碍(包括 疫苗接种 的 24 小时产后窗口期)的限制。专家呼吁提供综合产前三重筛查、政治和财政支持以及预防补贴,以缩小覆盖范围差距并实现世卫组织消除目标。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-08-09;事件日期:2022 年(垂直 传播 案例)和 2025 年(荟萃分析出版物)。
地点与对象
世界卫生组织非洲区域;接受产前护理的孕妇;2022年出生的婴儿。
事件
由医学博士 Peyton Thompson 与 MSCR 共同撰写的 2025 年《柳叶刀全球健康》杂志上发表的一项系统综述和荟萃分析量化了 WHO 非洲地区母婴 传播 (HBV) 传播 的负担,并讨论了消除垂直 传播 的策略。
规模、影响与风险
2022年估计有172,000例母婴传播乙型肝炎病毒(HBV)病例(占活产婴儿的0.4%);孕妇人群中乙型肝炎表面抗原(HBsAg)血清流行率为6.2%;4名慢性感染婴儿中有1名将因肝癌或肝脏相关并发症而早亡;2021年乙型肝炎疫苗出生剂次(HepB-BD)覆盖率为17%;将出生剂次覆盖率提高至90%可将母婴传播病例减少近44%,而结合出生剂次接种与孕妇抗病毒药物预防可将母婴传播病例减少86%以上。
应对、状态与不确定性
垂直 传播 可通过出生剂量 疫苗接种 和孕产妇 抗病毒药物 预防来预防;障碍包括 HepB-BD 覆盖率低(17%)、出生 24 小时内 疫苗 给药的后勤挑战、缺乏病毒载量检测、替诺福韦预防费用高昂(刚果民主共和国每月 25-30 美元)以及提供者教育有限;世卫组织建议从 2024 年开始,对资源匮乏地区的 HBsAg+ 孕妇进行孕产妇 抗病毒药物 预防;提议的解决方案是与艾滋病毒框架整合和替诺福韦补贴;建议进行产前三重筛查(HIV、HBV、梅毒),但并未普遍实施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Preventing Hepatitis B Vertical Transmission - Contagion Live

News Brief
A 2025 Lancet Global Health meta-analysis estimates 172,000 infants in the WHO African region acquired hepatitis B through mother-to-child transmission in 2022, with 6.2% of pregnant women HBsAg-positive. One in four chronically infected infants faces early death from liver cancer or complications. Current birth-dose vaccination coverage stands at just 17%, far below the 90% target needed to cut transmission by 44%. Combining vaccination with maternal antiviral prophylaxis could slash transmission by over 86%, yet access remains limited by cost—tenofovir costs $25–30 per month in the Democratic Republic of Congo—and logistical hurdles, including a 24-hour post-birth window for vaccination. Experts call for integrated prenatal triple screening, political and financial support, and subsidized prophylaxis to close the coverage gap and meet WHO elimination goals.
Original source text (English)
An estimated 172,000 infants acquired hepatitis B virus through mother-to-child transmission in the WHO African region in 2022, and combining birth-dose vaccination with maternal antiviral prophylaxis could cut this burden by more than 86%. Peyton Thompson, MD, MSCR, discusses the barriers to eliminating vertical transmission of hepatitis B, and strategies to reduce the coverage gap. Peyton Thompson, MD, MSCR Credit: UNC Hepatitis B virus continues to drive a heavy burden of chronic infection and early liver-related death among infants across the WHO African region, despite the availability of tools to prevent transmission at birth. A systematic review and meta-analysis published in Lancet Global Health estimated 172,000 cases of mother-to-child HBV transmission occurred in the region in 2022, accounting for 0.4% of all liveborn infants.1 Pooled HBsAg prevalence among pregnant individuals reached 6.2%, and 1 in 4 chronically infected infants will die prematurely from liver cancer or other liver-related complications.1 Raising timely birth-dose vaccination coverage to 90% could reduce vertical transmission by nearly 44%, and combining vaccination with maternal antiviral prophylaxis could lower transmission by more than 86%, though regional HepB-BD coverage remained at just 17% as of 2021.2 Peyton Thompson, MD, MSCR, assistant professor of pediatric infectious diseases at UNC School of Medicine and a member of UNC's Institute for Global Health and Infectious Diseases, co-authored the analysis. In the following interview, Thompson discusses the barriers to eliminating vertical transmission of hepatitis B in the WHO African region and the investments needed to close the coverage gap. Contagion: Your analysis found that an estimated 172,000 cases of mother-to-child hepatitis B transmission occurred in the WHO African region in 2022. What do these findings tell us about the current burden of vertical transmission, and why has this route of infection remained such a significant challenge? Thompson: In collaboration with a group of esteemed hepatitis B experts and led by Drs. Nicholas Riches and Alexander Stockdale, I co-authored a systematic review and meta-analysis of vertical transmission of HBV in the WHO African region, which was published in Lancet Global Health in 2025. 1 An estimated 172,000 cases of vertical transmission occurred in the African region in 2022, representing 0.4% of all liveborn infants. These infants who are infected with HBV will develop lifelong chronic infection, and 1 in 4 of them will die an early death from liver cancer or other liver-related complications. The current state of HBV control in Africa is unacceptable and represents profound health inequity. Vertical transmission of HBV is entirely preventable by combining birth-dose vaccine for babies plus antiviral prophylaxis for pregnant people, but these strategies remain inaccessible in many low-resource settings. Birth-dose vaccine has been introduced in nearly two-thirds of African countries, but overall coverage remains low at 17% due to logistical challenges in vaccine administration within 24 hours of birth. Unfortunately, birth-dose vaccine is insufficient to interrupt vertical transmission in cases of high-risk HBV (maternal viral load ≥200,000 IU/mL and/or HBeAg positivity), when antiviral prophylaxis is recommended to reduce the viral load. Maternal antiviral prophylaxis is now recommended for all HBsAg+ pregnant women in low-resource settings where viral load testing is not available, but is rarely provided. Accessibility of these life-saving and cancer-preventing resources is paramount to eliminating vertical transmission in the WHO African region. Contagion: The study suggests that increasing hepatitis B birth-dose vaccination coverage to 90% could reduce vertical transmission by nearly 44%, while combining vaccination with maternal antiviral prophylaxis could reduce transmission by more than 86%. What are the biggest barriers to implementing these interventions across the region, and how can they be overcome? Thompson: The barriers to timely birth-dose vaccination are complex and exist on multiple levels: policy (eg insufficient advocacy and affordability), facility (eg lack of knowledge among healthcare personnel, stock-outs, and the short window for vaccine administration) and community (eg lack of knowledge among caregivers and insufficient community health worker (CHW) engagement). 3 Educational campaigns for providers and caregivers, CHW involvement, political and financial support, and innovative strategies to reach out-of-facility births are among the strategies that can improve birth-dose coverage. Strategies must be adapted to the local context to enhance implementation. Barriers to maternal antiviral prophylaxis are related to the novelty and cost of this approach. Prophylaxis for all HBsAg+ pregnant women in low-resource settings was recommended by the WHO beginning in 2024, and so many countries have yet to adopt this strategy. Cost of tenofovir therapy hinders widespread uptake. In the Democratic Republic of Congo, a one-month course of tenofovir costs $25-30, nearly half of the average monthly income. Integration into the existing HIV framework could defray cost and implementation challenges. Ongoing education efforts for providers and patients and subsidization of prophylactic tenofovir are also necessary to overcome implementation barriers. Contagion: You found that approximately 6.2% of pregnant women attending antenatal care were hepatitis B surface antigen-positive. How should these findings influence prenatal screening policies and maternal care practices in countries across sub-Saharan Africa? Thompson: Indeed, the pooled HBV surface antigen (HBsAg) prevalence was 6.2% among pregnant individuals in the WHO African region. Prenatal screening for HBV is the first step in the pathway towards elimination of vertical transmission, but is still not performed in many low-resource settings. Triple screening for HIV, HBV and syphilis should co-occur prenatally, and there are now many examples of success stories using this approach. 4 Contagion: The study concludes that eliminating mother-to-child hepatitis B transmission in the WHO African region is achievable with expanded vaccination and antiviral use. What policy changes or investments should governments and global health organizations prioritize now to reach the WHO elimination targets? Thompson: The gap between current HepB-BD coverage (18% in the African region) and the WHO goal for elimination at 90% coverage is substantial. However, bridging this gap is possible with dedicated political will and financial investment. One solution to overcoming this gap is Gavi’s catalytic funding that provides HepB-BD free of charge (usually $0.30 per dose), alongside country-level commitment to vaccine administration and implementation. HepB-BD needs to be coupled with maternal antiviral prophylaxis to truly reach elimination targets of <0.1% HBsAg prevalence among children <5 years old. Global health organizations and individual countries in the WHO African region must incorporate both HepB-BD and maternal antiviral prophylaxis as a packaged approach, including educational initiatives, CHW engagement, cost subsidization, and coupling of care with HIV and syphilis towards triple elimination. Elimination of vertical transmission of HBV is at our fingertips. We need to come together as a global community to make it a reality. View five news elements
Time
Publication date: 2026-08-09; Event date: 2022 (vertical transmission cases) and 2025 (meta-analysis publication).
Location and population
WHO African region; pregnant individuals attending antenatal care; infants born in 2022.
Event
A systematic review and meta-analysis published in Lancet Global Health in 2025, co-authored by Peyton Thompson, MD, MSCR, quantified the burden of mother-to-child hepatitis B virus (HBV) transmission in the WHO African region and discussed strategies to eliminate vertical transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
172,000 estimated cases of mother-to-child HBV transmission in 2022 (0.4% of liveborn infants); 6.2% pooled HBsAg prevalence among pregnant individuals; 1 in 4 chronically infected infants will die prematurely from liver cancer or liver-related complications; current HepB-BD vaccination coverage at 17% (2021); raising birth-dose vaccination to 90% could reduce transmission by nearly 44%, while combining vaccination with maternal antiviral prophylaxis could reduce transmission by more than 86%.
Response, status and uncertainty
Vertical transmission is preventable via birth-dose vaccination and maternal antiviral prophylaxis; barriers include low HepB-BD coverage (17%), logistical challenges in vaccine administration within 24 hours of birth, lack of viral load testing, high cost of tenofovir prophylaxis ($25-30 per month in DRC), and limited provider education; WHO recommended maternal antiviral prophylaxis for HBsAg+ pregnant women in low-resource settings starting 2024; integration with HIV frameworks and subsidization of tenofovir are proposed solutions; prenatal triple screening (HIV, HBV, syphilis) is recommended but not universally implemented.
原文 / Source

随着乙型肝炎病例的减少,与酒精和脂肪肝相关的肝癌正在上升。如何预防? - 스포츠조선

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报韩国正在见证肝癌流行病学的重大转变,由于 疫苗接种 和 抗病毒药物 治疗的扩大,与 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 相关的病例有所下降,而与酒精相关和脂肪肝疾病相关的肝癌则急剧上升。根据 2023 年的登记数据,肝癌仍然是第七大常见癌症,但 死亡率 发病率在全国排名第二。五年生存率已提高至 40.4%,但仍低于总体癌症平均水平。专家将这一趋势归因于生活方式的改变和老龄化,目前乙型肝炎对新病例的贡献小于代谢性肝病和酒精性肝病。现在的预防重点是针对慢性 HBV/HCV 的 抗病毒药物 治疗、体重管理、代谢控制以及对高危人群进行每六个月的定期筛查。治疗的选择更加多样化,包括微创手术、AI辅助3D成像以及免疫治疗和靶向治疗等先进疗法,即使在晚期也能提供更好的治疗效果。

事件
关于韩国​​肝癌 流行病学 变化的指导文章,强调与 HBV 相关病例的减少以及与酒精和脂肪肝疾病相关的病例的增加。由朝鲜体育报报道,顺天乡大学富川医院伊曼纽尔教授进行了专家评论。

肝癌是韩国第七大常见癌症,在癌症类型中 死亡率 发病率排名第二。五年生存率从 20.6%(2001-2005 年)提高到 40.4%(2019-2023 年),但仍低于癌症总体平均水平 73.7%。慢性乙型肝炎 引起的肝癌比例有所下降,而由于生活方式的改变和人口老龄化,与酒精相关的肝病和代谢功能障碍相关的脂肪肝相关的病例正在增加。

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
As Hepatitis B Cases Decline, Liver Cancer Linked to Alcohol and Fatty Liver Is Rising. How Can It Be Prevented? - 스포츠조선
News Brief
South Korea is witnessing a significant epidemiological shift in liver cancer, with cases linked to hepatitis B virus (HBV) declining due to expanded vaccination and antiviral therapies, while alcohol-related and fatty liver disease-associated liver cancers are rising sharply. According to 2023 registry data, liver cancer remains the seventh most common cancer but has the second-highest mortality rate nationally. Five-year survival rates have improved to 40.4%, yet lag behind the overall cancer average. Experts attribute the trend to lifestyle changes and aging, with HBV now contributing less to new cases than metabolic and alcoholic liver disease. Prevention now focuses on antiviral treatment for chronic HBV/HCV, weight management, metabolic control, and regular six-month screening for high-risk groups. Treatment options have diversified, including minimally invasive surgery, AI-assisted 3D imaging, and advanced therapies such as immunotherapy and targeted treatments, offering better outcomes even in advanced stages.
Original source text (Korean)
Latest Celebrity K-Pop Drama Movie Culture Latest Celebrity K-Pop Drama Movie Culture Home Culture As Hepatitis B Cases Decline, Liver Cancer Linked to Alcohol and Fatty Liver Is Rising. How Can It Be Prevented? By Jongho, Jang Published 2026-08-10 15:40 Share this article X Facebook Copy Copy URL copied. File photo courtesy of Unsplash [Sportschosun Reporter Jang Jong-ho] According to the 2023 cancer registry statistics, liver cancer was the seventh most common cancer in South Korea, but it had the second-highest mortality rate among cancer types. The five-year survival rate has improved significantly from 20.6% in 2001-2005 to 40.4% in 2019-2023, but it is still below the overall cancer average of 73.7%. Professor Immanuel of the Department of Hepatobiliary and Pancreatic Surgery at Soonchunhyang University Bucheon Hospital explained, "Liver cancer is difficult to detect early because it usually causes no clear symptoms at first, and even when it has progressed significantly, it may only cause nonspecific symptoms such as indigestion, weight loss, loss of appetite, and fatigue. If liver function worsens, symptoms such as jaundice or ascites may also appear." ◇ The causes of liver cancer are shifting from viral hepatitis to fatty liver The main causes of liver cancer include chronic hepatitis B and C virus infections, cirrhosis, advanced metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver disease, and alcohol-related liver disease. As vaccinations and antiviral treatments have become more widely used, the share of liver cancer caused by chronic hepatitis B has declined, while the share linked to alcohol-related liver disease and metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver disease caused by obesity and diabetes has increased. With an aging population and changes in lifestyle, the number of fatty liver patients is rising, and this trend is expected to grow further. ◇ Personalized treatment should also take quality of life after treatment into account Liver cancer treatment is determined by a comprehensive assessment of not only the tumor's size and number, but also its location, whether major blood vessels are involved, liver function, the degree of cirrhosis, the patient's age, and overall condition. Treatment options include liver resection, liver transplantation, local therapies such as radiofrequency ablation, transarterial chemoembolization, radiation therapy, immunotherapy, and targeted therapy. Liver resection and liver transplantation are representative curative treatments that can offer the possibility of a cure, but even for liver cancer at the same stage, it is important to tailor the treatment to the patient based on liver function, tumor location, and overall condition. Liver resection is planned by evaluating both the amount and function of the remaining liver after surgery, so that enough function is preserved even after part of the liver is removed. The decision to operate is made after comprehensively considering whether the tumor is near major blood vessels, whether it is multifocal, whether there is metastasis outside the liver, and the patient's overall condition. If liver function is severely impaired and the risk of postoperative liver failure is high, or if the tumor meets liver transplantation criteria and cirrhosis is advanced, liver transplantation may be considered instead of liver resection. ◇ Minimally invasive liver surgery and AI-based 3D imaging make procedures more accurate and safer Recent liver cancer surgery has rapidly evolved toward reducing incisions while improving surgical precision and safety. In the past, most liver cancer operations were performed as open surgery, but recently the use of minimally invasive liver surgery with laparoscopy and robots has expanded. Because minimally invasive surgery is performed through several small incisions, it has the advantages of less postoperative pain, a smaller wound burden, and faster recovery. Recently, its use has also expanded to donor liver surgery in living-donor liver transplantation. At medical institutions with extensive experience, laparoscopic and robotic donor liver resection is performed when appropriate after fully evaluating the donor's vascular and biliary structures, the volume of the remaining liver, and surgical risks. Advances in imaging technology have also played an important role in improving the precision and safety of minimally invasive surgery. Soonchunhyang University Bucheon Hospital reconstructs preoperative CT and MRI images into AI-based 3D imaging to analyze the positions of tumors, blood vessels, and bile ducts in three dimensions. This allows doctors to accurately understand each patient's anatomical structure and predict the extent of resection and the volume of the remaining liver, enabling safer and more precise surgical planning. ◇ Prevention through antiviral treatment, weight control, management of metabolic disease, and regular screening To prevent liver cancer, patients with chronic hepatitis B or C need antiviral treatment, proper weight management, reduced alcohol consumption, regular exercise, and control of blood sugar and dyslipidemia. However, antiviral treatment does not completely eliminate the risk of liver cancer. If cirrhosis is already present or liver damage has progressed, liver cancer can still develop after treatment. Therefore, high-risk groups such as patients with chronic hepatitis B or C, cirrhosis, or advanced metabolic dysfunction-associated fatty liver disease need regular follow-up every six months, even if they have no special symptoms. Professor Immanuel said, "With recent major advances in treatment technology, the number of patients who can expect a cure when detected early is increasing, and a variety of treatment options are being developed that can deliver good outcomes even in advanced liver cancer." He added, "Because liver cancer usually does not cause special symptoms in its early stages, it is more important than anything else for high-risk individuals with liver cancer risk factors to undergo regular screening and detect it early, rather than waiting to visit the hospital after symptoms appear." How undiagnosed Hepatitis B is driving liver cancer rates in Africa - Daily Nation
新闻简报The article discusses how undiagnosed Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is driving liver cancer rates in Africa. It highlights that approximately 64 million people in Africa live with chronic HBV, which is the leading cause of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) in the region. HBV is responsible for about half of liver cancer cases in sub-Saharan Africa and up to 70-80% in younger adults in high-prevalence areas. The virus can remain asymptomatic for years, leading to severe complications like cirrhosis and liver failure. The article stresses the importance of early detection through blood tests and vaccination to prevent these outcomes. It also notes that HBV causes around 1.1 million deaths globally each year, mostly from cirrhosis and liver cancer. The piece calls for increased awareness and access to testing, as many individuals are unaware of their infection.

How undiagnosed Hepatitis B is driving liver cancer rates in Africa - Daily Nation

Renew subscription Maybe later Subscribe for a month to get full access How undiagnosed Hepatitis B is driving liver cancer rates in Africa Sunday, August 09, 2026 In Africa, where roughly 64 million people live with chronic hepatitis B, the infection remains the leading cause of liver cancer. Photo credit: Shutterstock By Dr Chris Opio Associate professor and consultant gastroenterologist & hepatologist Aga Khan University Medical College and Aga Khan University Hospital What you need to know: With Africa accounting for nearly 68 percent of new global infections, Hepatitis B remains a hidden danger that can damage the liver for decades without symptoms. You may feel perfectly healthy and still be living with hepatitis B without knowing it. The virus can quietly damage your liver for years, with symptoms sometimes appearing only after serious complications have developed. Although hepatitis B is preventable, it remains a leading cause of liver cancer and premature death worldwide. Much of this suffering can be avoided through vaccination, a simple blood test, and effective treatment. Hepatitis B is caused by the hepatitis B virus (HBV), one of five main viruses that cause hepatitis: A, B, C, D, and E. While some types cause only short-term illness, HBV can persist in the body for life. Over time, the chronic inflammation it triggers can scar the liver, potentially leading to cirrhosis, liver failure, and hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC), the most common type of primary liver cancer. Also read: I wasn't feeling sick, but I had Hepatitis B: The fight against a hiding virus The impact of hepatitis B is severe in Africa. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), around 240 million people globally were living with chronic hepatitis B in 2024, with roughly 64 million of them in Africa. That year, Africa accounted for nearly 68 percent of the estimated 900,000 new infections worldwide. Hepatitis B also caused about 1.1 million deaths, mostly from cirrhosis and liver cancer. Not everyone with chronic hepatitis B will develop cancer, but the link between HBV and liver cancer is among the strongest infection-cancer connections. The International Agency for Research on Cancer reported that hepatitis B caused about 380,000 cancer cases globally in 2020. In sub-Saharan Africa, hepatitis B remains the leading cause of hepatocellular carcinoma. Exact figures vary by country, but studies indicate that HBV is responsible for about half of liver cancer cases in the region. In high-prevalence areas, particularly in West Africa, it is linked to 70 to 80 percent of liver cancers in younger adults. This is why liver cancer in Africa often strikes at a younger age than in other parts of the world. The virus spreads through infected blood and certain body fluids. In regions where it is common, transmission most often occurs from an infected mother to her baby during birth or through close contact in early childhood. It can also spread via unprotected sex, needle or syringe sharing, unsafe medical injections, contaminated tattoo or piercing equipment, occupational exposure to infected blood, or sharing sharp personal items like razors. However, hepatitis B does not spread through everyday contact. You cannot catch it by sharing food or water, using the same utensils, breastfeeding, hugging, holding hands, coughing, or sneezing. People living with hepatitis B should not face stigma or discrimination. One of the biggest challenges of hepatitis B is its silence. It often produces no symptoms for many years. Like HIV, a person can look and feel perfectly healthy while the virus quietly harms their liver. When symptoms do appear, they may include yellowing of the eyes or skin (jaundice), dark urine, severe fatigue, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, joint pain, and flu-like symptoms. In rare cases, an acute infection can cause sudden, serious liver failure. Severe complications typically arise after decades of chronic infection. Advanced liver disease can cause a swollen abdomen due to fluid buildup (ascites), loss of appetite, weight loss, vomiting blood, confusion, malnutrition, kidney problems, cirrhosis, or liver failure. Waiting for these symptoms is risky, because advanced liver disease is much harder to treat. The best way to prevent serious complications is to detect the infection early. The only reliable way to know whether someone has hepatitis B is through a blood test. Symptoms alone cannot confirm the infection or distinguish it from other liver problems. Blood tests can show whether the infection is recent or chronic, whether a person has developed immunity after vaccination, or whether a previous infection has cleared. Additional tests can measure the amount of virus in the blood and assess the risk of disease progression and transmission. Doctors can also use tools like ultrasound, FibroScan, and other imaging scans to check whether the liver has developed significant scarring. Testing is especially important because many people do not know they are infected. In 2024, only about 65 million people, roughly 27 per cent of those with chronic hepatitis B worldwide, knew their status. Pregnant women should be tested, because identifying infection allows for interventions that can dramatically reduce transmission to newborns. Household contacts, sexual partners of infected individuals, healthcare workers, and people exposed to infected blood should also consider testing. Also read: Prevention and treatment of Hepatitis B Early treatment of hepatitis B can prevent liver cancer. Most people with uncomplicated acute hepatitis B do not require specific antiviral medicine, as many recover on their own. However, chronic hepatitis B can be managed effectively with oral antiviral drugs. This treatment reduces the risk of cirrhosis and lowers the likelihood of liver cancer. For many patients, treatment is long-term and may be lifelong. It is important to remember that antiviral treatment does not replace regular check-ups. People with chronic hepatitis B still have a risk of liver cancer, especially if they have cirrhosis or other risk factors, so they need ongoing medical care. Vaccination remains the best protection against hepatitis B. Every child should receive the first dose of the hepatitis B vaccine as soon as possible after birth, ideally within 24 hours, followed by the remaining doses according to the national immunization schedule. Children, teenagers, and adults who have not been vaccinated should also complete the full recommended series. Babies born to mothers with hepatitis B need urgent protection, and mothers with high viral levels may require antiviral treatment during pregnancy to reduce the risk of transmission. Completing the full hepatitis B vaccine series provides more than 95 percent protection. Being diagnosed with hepatitis B does not mean someone cannot live a normal, productive life. People with the infection can continue working, studying, and participating in family and community activities. They should maintain regular medical check-ups, eat a healthy diet, exercise regularly, avoid alcohol, and consult a doctor before using any herbal or over-the-counter medicines, since some products can harm the liver. They may also need testing for other viral hepatitis types, such as hepatitis C, and may require the hepatitis A vaccine if recommended. Hepatitis B can quietly damage the liver, but liver cancer from HBV does not have to happen. Vaccination can stop the infection. Testing can find those at risk. Treatment can slow the disease and save lives. The problem is not that we lack solutions. The real challenge is ensuring that people know their status and can access prevention and care. A simple vaccine, a blood test, and timely treatment can prevent lifelong liver disease. Dr Opio is associate professor, Aga Khan University Medical College and consultant gastroenterologist & hepatologist at Aga Khan University Hospital Login to join the discussion Please enable JavaScript to view the comments powered by Disqus. It causes around 1.1 million deaths annually, mostly from cirrhosis and liver cancer.
Response, status and uncertainty
The article highlights the importance of early detection through blood tests and vaccination to prevent serious complications. It also emphasizes the need for awareness and access to testing, as many people are unaware of their infection.

关于乙型肝炎,家庭应该了解什么 - 硅谷观察家

What Families Should Know About Hepatitis B - Silicon Valley Watcher
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 10 日,《硅谷观察者》发表的一篇指导文章强调了随着返校季节的临近,乙型肝炎 疫苗 对儿童的重要性。这篇文章由住院医生 Laura Romano,D.O. 撰写,澄清乙型肝炎是一种由 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 引起的严重肝脏疾病,它可以通过接触受感染的体液传播,包括在分娩期间或通过牙刷等共用物品传播。婴幼儿特别容易受到慢性感染,这可能导致严重的肝脏疾病,如肝硬化或肝癌。文章强调,疫苗接种 是最有效的预防方法,提供约 95% 的保护,并指出全民婴儿免疫接种减少了乙型肝炎病例和相关癌症。建议家庭咨询儿科医生,通过库克儿童医院安排预约,或联系塔兰特县公共卫生部门获取免疫资源。

规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告。该文章讨论了 慢性乙型肝炎 导致肝损伤、肝硬化和肝癌的风险,特别是在婴儿和幼儿中,但没有提供病例数、死亡数或暴露数。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

What Families Should Know About Hepatitis B - Silicon Valley Watcher

News Brief
On August 10, 2026, a guidance article published by Silicon Valley Watcher emphasized the importance of the hepatitis B vaccine for children as back-to-school season approaches. Authored by Laura Romano, D.O., a hospitalist, the piece clarifies that hepatitis B is a serious liver disease caused by the hepatitis B virus (HBV), which can spread through contact with infected bodily fluids, including during childbirth or through shared items like toothbrushes. Infants and young children are particularly vulnerable to chronic infection, which may lead to severe liver conditions such as cirrhosis or liver cancer. The article underscores that vaccination is the most effective prevention method, offering about 95% protection, and notes that universal infant immunization has reduced hepatitis B cases and associated cancers. Families are advised to consult their pediatrician, schedule appointments through Cook Children's, or contact Tarrant County Public Health for immunization resources.
Original source text (English)
Fort Worth, August 10 , 2026 | 08:00 AM America/Chicago What Families Should Know About Hepatitis B Back-to-School Vaccine Breakdown: Protecting Your Child’s Health with the Hepatitis B Vaccine Written by: Laura Romano, D.O. Resources and tips for finding immunizations for children: Navigating vaccines can feel overwhelming. Talk to your pediatrician. Schedule an appointment through Cook Children's . Find your nearest Texas Department of State Health service clinic for information on clinics. Call 211 for help. Call Tarrant County Public Health at 817-248-6299 to make an appointment or to find a walk-in clinic. Check with your local school district for vaccine events. Raising a child brings up endless questions, especially when it comes to their health and well-being. As back-to-school season approaches, immunizations may be top of mind for families, and searching for clear, honest answers about what goes into their child's body and why. With so much conflicting information online, sorting facts from fear can feel overwhelming. Cook Children’s Health Care System helps parents and caregivers cut through the noise with trusted guidance from medical experts who are parents themselves. Among the required school shots, the Hepatitis B vaccine often raises the most questions, especially when it comes to why infants and young kids need protection so early. Hospitalist Laura Romano, D.O. , explains the key facts about hepatitis B and the vaccine that prevents it. Hospitalist Laura Romano, D.O., What is Hepatitis B? Hepatitis B is a serious liver disease that is caused by the hepatitis B virus (HBV). In adults, hepatitis B is usually spread through blood or intimate contact. In young children, however, the virus usually spreads through contact with infected bodily fluids. Transmission most commonly occurs during labor and delivery, but it can also happen through everyday activities, such as sharing a toothbrush or being bitten by a person who has the virus. People infected with hepatitis B can spread the virus even if they show no symptoms. In fact, children often remain asymptomatic for years while still being contagious. This is especially concerning because infants and young children are more likely than adults to develop chronic, lifelong liver conditions, including permanent liver damage, cirrhosis (severe scarring), and liver cancer. Treatment options for chronic hepatitis B are limited and focus primarily on halting the progression of liver damage rather than curing the virus itself. For severe cases where the liver has sustained significant damage, a liver transplant may ultimately be necessary. How can the disease be prevented? The most effective way to prevent hepatitis B is through vaccination. The vaccine is highly protective, offering about 95% effectiveness in preventing both acute and chronic infections. Since its routine inclusion in infant immunization schedules, rates of hepatitis B cases and associated liver cancer have steadily declined. What would you like families to know about the hepatitis B vaccine and HBV? Hepatitis B is often misunderstood as a disease that only affects people engaging in high-risk or illegal behaviors. Anyone can contract the virus through everyday actions such as sharing a razor or toothbrush with someone who has the infection, even if that person shows no symptoms. Prenatal screening and maternal immunity testing alone are not enough to protect all children from hepatitis B. History has shown that targeting only 'high-risk' infants leaves gaps, leading to ongoing infections in both mothers and children that universal vaccination prevents. While some countries delay hepatitis B vaccination until children are older, their social and health care infrastructure differs significantly. Longer parental leave in those countries means children spend less time in daycare, where early exposure can occur. Also, universal health care access allows these countries to detect acute infections quickly and manage chronic cases more effectively. Related Stories: Mindful Monday Medicine: Back to School Checklist Prioritizing Firearm Safety and Youth Well-being in Fort Worth Heat Safety for Kids: Hydration, Heat Illness Signs and Sun Protection Tips

Published on 2026-08-10 at 08:00 AM America/Chicago (event date not specified; guidance is current as of publication).

Fort Worth, Texas, USA; families with children preparing for school; pediatric population and general public.
Event
Guidance article explaining hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, transmission, risks, and prevention via vaccination, authored by Laura Romano, D.O., a hospitalist. The article clarifies misconceptions and emphasizes the importance of the hepatitis B vaccine for infants and young children.
Scale, impact and risk
Not reported in the supplied evidence. The article discusses the risk of chronic hepatitis B leading to liver damage, cirrhosis, and liver cancer, particularly in infants and young children, but does not provide case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers.
Response, status and uncertainty
The article provides prevention guidance through vaccination and directs families to resources for scheduling immunizations, including Cook Children's Health Care System, Tarrant County Public Health, and local school districts. It highlights the effectiveness of the vaccine (approximately 95% protection) and the need for universal vaccination to prevent gaps in protection. No investigation or outbreak response is described.

病毒生物标志物指南 慢性乙型肝炎 的个性化 功能性治愈 策略 [IMAGE] - EurekAlert!

Viral Biomarkers Guide Personalized Functional Cure Strategies for Chronic Hepatitis B [IMAGE] - EurekAlert!
新闻简报这篇新闻报道由 EurekAlert! 于 2026 年 8 月 10 日发表,重点介绍了由中国福建医科大学欧其水教授领导的研究,该研究涉及使用病毒生物标记物来指导 慢性乙型肝炎 的个性化 功能性治愈 策略。该研究探讨了如何将经典和新兴的 乙型肝炎病毒 生物标志物(例如定量 HBsAg、HBV DNA、HBcrAg、HBV RNA 和定量抗 HBc)整合到治疗前分层、治疗 监测、核苷(酸)类似物停药和 治疗 后巩固的临床框架中。该报告没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露数量,也没有提及任何调查、测试或 治疗 行动。它是部分内容摘要,重点关注研究的科学意义,而不是 暴发 或公共卫生事件。这些发现与人类 乙型肝炎病毒 感染以及 HBV 相关肝细胞癌和 HBV 相关肝硬化等相关病症相关。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Viral Biomarkers Guide Personalized Functional Cure Strategies for Chronic Hepatitis B [IMAGE] - EurekAlert!

News Brief
This news report, published on August 10, 2026, by EurekAlert!, highlights research led by Professor Qishui Ou from Fujian Medical University, China, on the use of viral biomarkers to guide personalized functional cure strategies for chronic hepatitis B. The study explores how classical and emerging hepatitis B virus biomarkers, such as quantitative HBsAg, HBV DNA, HBcrAg, HBV RNA, and quantitative anti-HBc, can be integrated into a clinical framework for pretreatment stratification, treatment monitoring, nucleos(t)ide analogue discontinuation, and post-treatment consolidation. The report does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, nor does it mention any investigation, testing, or treatment actions. It is a partial content summary and focuses on the scientific implications of the research rather than an outbreak or public health event. The findings are relevant to human hepatitis B virus infection and related conditions such as HBV-associated hepatocellular carcinoma and HBV-related cirrhosis.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Aligos 开始在中国测试 慢性乙型肝炎 候选者 - Stock Titan

Aligos Begins Testing a Chronic Hepatitis B Candidate in China - Stock Titan
新闻简报Aligos Therapeutics, a clinical-stage biopharmaceutical company, announced on August 12, 2026, that its partner Amoytop has initiated the Phase 1 study of ALG-170675, an investigational antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) for chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, in China. The trial aims to evaluate the drug's safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics using single and multiple ascending doses in healthy participants, followed by a cohort of chronic HBV patients. The study includes four single-dose cohorts (75, 150, 300, and 450 mg) and a multiple-dose phase, with the chronic HBV cohort expected to begin in Q4 2026 after completing the 150 mg multiple-ascending-dose phase. ALG-170675 has shown improved RNase H-mediated in vivo activity compared to GSK-836 (bepirovirsen) in preclinical testing, and the trial is part of ongoing efforts to develop a functional cure for HBV. No confirmed cases, deaths, or exposures are reported in the supplied evidence, and the findings remain preliminary.

Aligos Therapeutics announced the initiation of a Phase 1 study of ALG-170675, an investigational antisense oligonucleotide for chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection, with its partner Amoytop.

该研究涉及 32 名健康受试者的单次剂量递增 (SAD) 试验和 16 名受试者的慢性 HBV 队列研究 试验,以安全性、耐受性、药代动力学和药效学作为关键评估领域。所提供的证据中没有报告确诊病例、死亡或暴露情况。

Key Terms antisense oligonucleotide, pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, rnase h 4 terms antisense oligonucleotide medical "investigational next-generation antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) being evaluated" An antisense oligonucleotide is a small piece of synthetic genetic material designed to attach to specific molecules in the body’s cells, effectively blocking or modifying how genes are expressed. This technology is important because it can be used to develop targeted treatments for certain diseases, which may influence the value of biotech companies and the broader healthcare sector. Its development reflects advances in personalized medicine and gene-based therapies. pharmacokinetics medical "evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics" Pharmacokinetics is the study of how a substance, such as a drug or chemical, moves through and is processed by the body over time. It tracks how it is absorbed, distributed, broken down, and eventually eliminated. For investors, understanding pharmacokinetics helps gauge the effectiveness, safety, and potential risks of new medications or treatments, which can influence a company’s success and valuation in the healthcare industry. pharmacodynamics medical "evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics" Pharmacodynamics is how a drug actually affects the body — the strength, type and duration of its effects and the relationship between dose and response. Think of it like how turning a thermostat changes room temperature: it shows what the drug does and how much is needed to get the desired effect. Investors care because these properties drive clinical success, dosing convenience, safety profile and competitive advantage, all of which influence commercial potential and regulatory approval. rnase h technical "improved RNase H mediated in vivo activity over GSK-836" RNase H is an enzyme that recognizes and cuts the RNA strand of an RNA–DNA hybrid, helping cells remove RNA primers during DNA replication and process RNA bound to DNA. For investors, it matters because RNase H activity is a key mechanism targeted or leveraged by certain genetic and antiviral therapies, diagnostics, and lab tools; its presence or inhibition can determine how some drugs work or how biotechnologies are developed. Adds StockTitan as a preferred source · opens Google Add on Google Not now 08/12/2026 - 08:05 PM ALG-170675 has been optimized for both HBsAg RNA inhibition and immuno-activation Single and multiple ascending doses planned Study will allow rapid advancement into participants with chronic HBV infection in Q4 2026 SOUTH SAN FRANCISCO, Calif., Aug. 12, 2026 (GLOBE NEWSWIRE) -- Aligos Therapeutics, Inc. (Nasdaq: ALGS), a clinical stage biotechnology company focused on improving patient outcomes through best-in-class therapies for liver and viral diseases, today announced that dosing has been initiated in the Phase 1 study of ALG-170675, an investigational next-generation antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) being evaluated for functional cure in the treatment of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection. The study is being conducted in China by Xiamen Amoytop Biotech Co., Ltd. (“Amoytop”), who maintain rights in Greater China. “Dosing the first subject in the Phase 1 study of ALG-170675 represents a significant milestone for Aligos and reflects our commitment to advancing potentially best-in-class therapies for patients living with chronic HBV infection,” stated Lawrence Blatt, Ph.D., M.B.A., Chairman, President, and Chief Executive Officer of Aligos Therapeutics. “Despite currently available treatments, chronic HBV infection remains a major global health challenge, with millions of patients at risk of developing cirrhosis, liver failure, and hepatocellular carcinoma. ALG-170675 was designed as a next-generation antisense oligonucleotide with the potential to enhance functional cure rates for patients living with chronic HBV infection, and its advancement into clinical development represents an important step toward that goal. We are highly appreciative of the exceptional efforts of our partner Amoytop, whose expertise, commitment, and efficient execution have enabled the rapid advancement of ALG-170675 into the clinic in China. We look forward to continuing our successful collaboration as the study progresses, including the planned evaluation of ALG-170675 in participants with chronic HBV infection later this year. Looking ahead, we believe there is potential to further improve functional cure rates through combination regimens that may include Amoytop’s pegylated interferon alfa product, PEGBING®, as well as pevifoscorvir sodium, our potentially best-in-class capsid assembly modulator licensed to Amoytop in Greater China. Together, these programs underscore our shared commitment to advancing innovative therapies that address the substantial unmet needs of patients living with chronic HBV infection worldwide.” ALG-170675 is a next-generation ASO discovered by Aligos as part of a research collaboration with Amoytop, who retain rights in Greater China. Novel intellectual property has been filed for this drug candidate, which has shown improved RNase H mediated in vivo activity over GSK-836 (bepirovirsen) with similar hTLR8 agonist activity observed in vitro and in vivo. In addition, ALG-170675 utilizes novel monomers that could potentially reduce ASO toxicity and improve ASO liver to kidney ratios. “We are pleased to have initiated dosing in the Phase 1 study of ALG-170675 in China, marking an important step in the clinical development of this promising investigational therapy for chronic HBV infection,” said Sun Li, Chairman and Chief Executive Officer at Amoytop. “This milestone reflects the strong collaboration between Amoytop and Aligos, as well as our shared commitment to advancing innovative therapies to address the significant unmet need for patients living with chronic HBV infection.” The Phase 1 study is designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of ALG-170675 following single (SAD) and multiple (MAD) ascending doses. The SAD portion plans to enroll approximately 32 healthy participants across four dose cohorts of 75 mg, 150 mg, 300 mg, and 450 mg. Following completion of the 300 mg SAD cohort, the MAD portion of the study is expected to begin and would enroll approximately 24 healthy participants. The study design also includes a cohort of approximately 16 participants with chronic HBV infection, which is expected to initiate following completion of the 150 mg MAD cohort in Q4 2026. For more information, please visit the Chinese Clinical Trial Register. About Aligos Aligos Therapeutics, Inc. (NASDAQ: ALGS) is a clinical stage biopharmaceutical company founded with the mission to improve patient outcomes by developing best-in-class therapies for the treatment of liver and viral diseases. Aligos applies its science driven approach and deep R&D expertise to advance its purpose-built pipeline of therapeutics with high unmet medical needs such as chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) infection. For more information, please visit www.aligos.com or follow us on LinkedIn or X. About Xiamen Amoytop Biotech Co., Ltd. Xiamen Amoytop Biotech Co., Ltd. is an innovative biopharmaceutical company and a listed company on the Science and Technology Innovation Board (SSE STAR Market) in China, specializing in R&D, manufacturing and marketing of regular and long-acting recombinant protein drugs. Focusing on the R&D of immune-related cytokine medicines, Amoytop is committed to becoming a leader in solving cytokine medicine-based systemic immune problems, providing better solutions for major diseases (such as viral hepatitis, malignant tumors) and immunotherapy. Aligos Therapeutics announced first dosing in the Phase 1 study of ALG-170675, conducted by Amoytop in China. According to Aligos, this trial evaluates safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of the antisense oligonucleotide for potential functional cure of chronic hepatitis B virus infection. What is ALG-170675 in the Aligos Therapeutics (ALGS) pipeline and what makes it distinctive? ALG-170675 is an investigational next-generation antisense oligonucleotide targeting chronic HBV infection. According to Aligos, it has shown improved RNase H–mediated in vivo activity over GSK-836 (bepirovirsen), similar hTLR8 agonist activity, and uses novel monomers that may reduce toxicity and improve liver-to-kidney distribution. How is the Phase 1 clinical trial of ALG-170675 by Amoytop in China designed? The Phase 1 trial includes single and multiple ascending dose phases in healthy participants, plus an HBV cohort. According to Aligos, the single-dose part plans ~32 participants across 75, 150, 300 and 450 mg cohorts, followed by a multiple-dose phase with ~24 participants. Arbutus 乙型肝炎药物进入拟议的 2b 期试验 - Stock Titan

Arbutus Hepatitis B Drug Moves Toward Proposed Phase 2b Trial - Stock Titan
新闻简报Arbutus Biopharma, a U.S.-based company, announced in May 2026 that it has reached alignment with the FDA on a proposed Phase 2b trial design for its drug imdusiran in the treatment of chronic hepatitis B. The trial involves 10 patients and is part of the company's ongoing efforts to develop a precision medicine approach using RNAi technology. In the second quarter of 2026, Arbutus reported financial results, including a $178.4M payment from Moderna and a $179.4M receivable from Genevant. The company also mentioned plans to return up to $230M to shareholders via potential share repurchases, though this remains conditional on board approval. The report highlights the company's financial position, with $92.6M in cash and marketable securities as of June 30, 2026. However, the contingent settlement payment of $1.3B is dependent on an appellate ruling, and the trial's progress is still under regulatory review. The FDA's Fast Track designation for imdusiran in April 2026 indicates its potential significance in the treatment of chronic hepatitis B, but no explicit risk level or impact on public health is provided in the report.

时间
The news report was published on 2026-08-12. The event date is May 2026, when Arbutus reached alignment with the FDA on a proposed Phase 2b trial design for imdusiran in chronic hepatitis B. The financial results were reported for the second quarter of 2026.
地点与对象
该报告重点关注美国 Arbutus Biopharma 公司。该试验设计针对的是影响人类的慢性乙型肝炎,但没有提及具体的地理位置或人口规模。
事件
Arbutus Biopharma announced a proposed Phase 2b trial design for imdusiran in chronic hepatitis B, in alignment with the FDA. The company also reported second quarter 2026 financial results, including a $178.4M payment from Moderna and a $179.4M receivable from a Genevant license.
规模、影响与风险
The trial design involves 10 patients treated with imdusiran. The financial impact includes a $92.6M cash balance, a $178.4M Moderna payment, and a $179.4M receivable from Genevant. The company reported a net loss of $5.1M in Q2 2026. The FDA Fast Track designation and regulatory alignment are noted, but no explicit risk level is stated.
应对、状态与不确定性
拟议的 2b 期试验设计正在与 FDA 讨论。通过股票回购获得的股东回报取决于董事会的批准。$1.3B 的或有和解付款取决于上诉裁决。该报告没有提供有关该药物的调查或测试状态的进一步细节。

Key Terms lipid nanoparticle (lnp), fast track designation, rnai, hbsag, +1 more 5 terms lipid nanoparticle (lnp) technical "patent enforcement lawsuits against Pfizer and BioNTech related to lipid nanoparticle (LNP) technology" A lipid nanoparticle (LNP) is a tiny, oil-like shell made from fats that carries fragile molecules such as RNA or drugs into the body, acting like a microscopic delivery truck or protective bubble. It matters to investors because LNPs determine whether advanced therapies and vaccines work, how safe they are, how difficult they are to manufacture and scale, and therefore influence product value, regulatory approval chances, and commercial potential. fast track designation regulatory "the FDA granted Fast Track designation for imdusiran" Fast track designation is a status the U.S. Food and Drug Administration grants to drugs intended to treat serious conditions and address an unmet medical need. It gives the developer more frequent communication with the FDA and can allow parts of the application to be reviewed on a rolling basis, and it may pave the way to priority review or accelerated approval. It can shorten development timelines, though it does not guarantee approval. rnai medical "Imdusiran is an RNAi therapeutic specifically designed to reduce" RNAi, short for RNA interference, is a natural cellular process that acts like a mute button for specific genes by using small RNA molecules to stop a target gene from producing its protein. For investors, RNAi matters because it is the underlying mechanism behind a class of precision medicines that can silence disease-causing genes, offering high potential returns but also scientific, regulatory and manufacturing risks unique to novel drug platforms. hbsag medical "including hepatitis B surface antigen (“HBsAg”)" HBsAg is a protein that appears on the surface of the hepatitis B virus and is detected by medical tests to show whether someone currently carries or is infectious with hepatitis B. For investors, HBsAg matters because it is a primary diagnostic and regulatory marker used in drug and vaccine trials, blood screening, and public-health programs; shifts in testing, treatment, or prevalence can affect the commercial prospects and approval pathways for related medical products. hbv dna medical "hepatitis B virus deoxyribonucleic acid (“HBV DNA”)" HBV DNA is the genetic material of the hepatitis B virus measured in a blood sample to show how much virus is present. For investors, changes in HBV DNA levels drive clinical trial outcomes, drug approvals, and market expectations for treatments or diagnostics—think of it as a thermometer reading that tells whether an antiviral is lowering the viral “load” and therefore whether a therapy is working.
